    ~~                                                    6 OCT 69

  ~~~                 2.          At approximately 2:40 A.M. Central Daylight savings time
                      I was proceeding North on P.T.H. #83 a~ approximately 45 M. P. H••
                      I noticed a light, the approximate size of a tower clearance light,
                      in the s.k y, to the North East. The light appeared to be almost
                      directly North of Napinka, Manitoba. The light started to get larger
                      and at this time I noticed it was moving in a Westerly direction
                      towards P.T . H. #83 . I glanced at my watch and the time was exactly
                      2:45A . M•• The object appeared to be moving at appro~mately the
                      same speed as I was travelling and it appeared that our paths would
                      cross on P.T.H. #SJ approximately 6 miles NC!lrth of Melita, '''anitoba.
                      While going North my view was momentarily obstructed by trees on the
                      East side of the road. Once I bad a clear view again the object
                      had stopped. Its location at this time appeared to be over the
                      S/W t 32-4- 26 Rural Municipality of Arthur , Manitoba, which is
                      approximately 6 miles North of Melita. I stopped the car on the
                      side of the road, turned off the lights and ignition and got out
                      of the car. At this time I had a chance to make the following
                      observations. The object was perfectly round and the bottom half
                      was lighted~ the upper half seemed to be blacked out with only a
                      thin line or light around it. The bottom half was a yellowish
                      red and seemed to be o! an illuminous nature. It did not cast any
                      light on the ground or on any surrounding objects. I did not hear
                      any sound and did not notice anything unusual in the immediate area.
                      The object at this time was about twice the size of a full moon,
                      it appeared to be a perfect circle with no ragged edges or glow
                      surrounding it. The object stayed in t his position for approximately
                      45 seconds and then started to move t o t he east retracing its path.
                      The object diminished in size equal to that when it was first
                      sighted and faded into cloud cover, The t ime the object was last
                      seen was 2:49A. M.
                      J,        The weather at the time of the sighting was a heavy
                      overcast s~y, with a ceiling of approximately 1 1 000 f eet. Earlier
                      in the evening the area in question had a light rain, however
                      there was no rain falli ng at the time of t he sighting . Visibility
                      was approximately 5 miles, the wind was a dead cal m.

                 It was about 9:45 P.M. when -    and I were driving West
    on PR 11469. We were approximately two mries West of Hamiota when
    we first saw it. As we drove along, we saw a huge object, sort of
    an orange, reddish colour. It was circular in shape and seemed to
    be blotted out by the trees as it moved away. We only watched it
    for three or four minutes. As we watched it, we noticed it glowed.
    It wa.s on the South side or the road when we saw it and it moved
    away from u.s in a south-westerly direction.
    Wit:
         Cat . J.A. COAGIE                     Sgd.
    Q.UESTIONS and ANSWERS :
    Q: On what day and· at approximately what time did you see the object?
    A. I can't remember the day, but it was about 9 :45 P. M.
    Q, What lt'aS the    condition of the sky at the time you saw it?
    A. I can't remember,
    Q. Exactly where were you when rou saw it?
    A. We were approximately two mi es West of. Ramiota, on Provincial
       Road 11469 proceeding in a westerly direction.
    Q. Were there any bursts from this object?
    A, No.
    Q. How bright was the object that you saw?
    A. It was brighter than the moon.
    Q, What colour was the object?
    A. It was a reddish, orange colour.
    Q. Did it leave a trail or colour or smoke behind it?
    A, No.
    Q. Was it dark at the time you saw it?
    A. Yes.
    Q.How big was the object?
    A. It was much higher than the trees .
    Q. From where you were, how big was the object in comparison to the
       moon?
    A. It was a good twice the size.
    Q. How long did you see the object in motion?
    A. All the time we saw it. Three or four minutes.
    Q. Did this object emit any sounds?

         Q. Where was the object when you first saw it?
         A. It was just to the South side of the road and seemed to be behind
            this one clump of trees.
         Q. What type of a path did the object follow?
         A. It was low and moved away from us in a south-westerly direction.
         Q. Was the object at any time up in the sky?
         A. No. It was close to the ground.
         Q. Was there anything else unusual that you noticed about the object?
         A. No

              It was either on the 16th or 17th of September, about
  10:00 P.M. , approximately two miles We at of Hamiota, when we first
  saw it. As we were going West, we saw a big, orangish, red ball,
  about twice the size of the moon, behind some trees, It seemed to
   move out and keep ahead or ua as we dron along. We watched it for
  three or four minutes as we drove along at 30 miles per hour. It
  then disappeared.
  Wit:
         Cst, J,A, COAGIE                         Sgd.
  QUESTIONS and ANSWERS:
  Q. On what day and at approximately what time did you see the object?      :,t<.··
  A. It was either the 16th or 17th of September, around 10:00 .P.M. ·   · · '"'.f:.
  Q. What was the condition of the sky at the time?
  A. I'm pretty sure it was clear.
  Q. Was it dark at the time?
  A. Yea.
  Q. Exactly where were you when you saw the object?
  A. I was two miles West of Hamiota, on Provincial ~oad #~69
     going in a westerly direction.
  Q. Were there any bursts of colour from this object?
  A. No, It just glowed in itself.
  Q. How bright was the object that you saw?
  A. I thought it was the moon at first, but I guess it wasn't.   It
  was almost the same brightness,
  Q. What colour was the object?
  A, An orangish red.
  Q. Did it leave a trail of colour or smoke behind it?
  A. No,
  Q, How big was the object?
  A. I'd say, about two or three times the size of the moon .
  Q. How long was the object in motion?
  A, About three or four minutes, until the time I lost sight of it and
  it went into the bushes.
  Q. While you were watching the object, did you hear any strange sounds?
  A. No. There was no sound at all.

           On Saturday, 9 AUG 69 at around 11 :00 AM, I was at a pond
           with 1111 eon, - . TMs pond is oft the T.c. Highway about
           10 miles from-gr. John's, Nfld. I was fishing and my son
           yelled to me to look at what was coming dmn1. I looked up
           in the sk7 and saw this object falling from the sky. When
           I first saw it, it was about 1000 feet or more up in the eky.
           It was about the aile of a small plane. This object appeared
           to be zig tagging down and looked round. There was a tail of
           smoke coming from it till it went out of sight behind a bill
           about 5 lliles or so away. It appeared a reddish orange in
           colour and there was a light glistening off it. Like the .
           sua was reflecting off it. From the fir$t time l saw i~ . ~~i       •
           it went out of sight behind this hill would be about 5 min~tes
           or more. I did not time it but I would guess this was how · .
           long I saw h. It was coming down quite fast. There appeared
           to be a flame coming from the end of it and then the tail' of
        ~ smoke or something resembling smoke.    The weather this day was
   -    ~ clear and good visibility.    I am a retired Telecommunations
      tic~ radio operator. This is the first time I have ever seen         ..
           anything like this. When I first saw this object I assumed         •·
      ~    that someone was firing rockets. Later, I reported t his to
    zi~ ~ the R.C.M. Police in St. John's. I did not hear any noise
     I·<   from this object.

                                                                             August 22, 1969


                         Inspector W. P. G. Perry
                         A/Officer in Charge
                         Criminal Investigation Branch
                         Royal Canadian Mounted Police
                         Ottawa 7, Ontario
                         Dear Inspector Perry:

                                              This will acknowledge receipt of the
                         specimens of grass which I gather are relevant to the
                         unidentified flying object seen on the Hunt Club Road. I
                         will be having th.is material examined shortly, but am very
                         doubtful that it has any connection with material from outer
                         space. Thank you for your co-operation in forwarding it to
                         IRe,


                                                         Sincerely yours,


                                                         Peter M. Millman
                                                         Head of Upper Atmosphere Research

                                 NATIONAL RESEARCH COUNCIL OF CANADA
                               CONSEIL NATIONAL DE RECHERCHES DU CANADA

                                                                        O TTAWA 7.

                                                                    16 October 1969
                   Inspector W.F.G. Perry,
                   A/Officer in Charge,
                   Criminal Investigation Branch,
                   Royal Canadian ~founted Police,
                   Ottawa 7, Ont.
                   Dear Inspector Perry:
                        Re my letter of August 22, your letter of August 1 and the
                   U.F.O. llunt Club Road Report dated August 11, HQ-400-Q-S (Vo1.4),
                   69A-400-214 , 69-400-255.
                         I have had the grass sample studied at the Geological Survey
                   in Ottawa. The grass was given X-ray analysis and the crystalline
                   substance showed no evidence of a mineral content or any type of
                   radio-activity. These results indicate that it was some type of
                   biological mould of which there are many varieties and these may
                   frequently assume a circular pattern. 1 might note the well-known
                   mushroom rings found in Ireland and other parts of the world. Since
                   no other phenomenon or aerial sighting was associated with this instance,
                   I see no reason to pursue thh further. Thank you for your assistance
                   in calling this to our attention.          '
                                                           Sincerely yours,


                                                           Peter M. Millman,
                                                           Head,
                                                           Upper Atmosphere Research.

~~cr:    1·         At 11:00 Al-i this date, ~. who farms 8 miles
~    ~   South of Assiniboia 'phoned this ~orted that he had
~    9   sighted an unidentified flying object in the sky at 2:56 A.\1 c . s . T.
         from the steps of his home. He stated it was in the shape of a round
         ball With a cone-shaped attachment to the ball, followed by white
         lines which appear to be an exhaust. The ball was a bright cherry-
         red, cone being a bluish white with white exhaust lines.
         2.           A patrol was made to the farm and the folloWing statement
         obtained:
                      STATE!~T    of                                     ASSINIBOIAL_SASKATC~~AN
                                                                         TAKEN: 19 JUL 69
                     non Saturday morning, July 19th at 4 minutes to 3, I was
         standing on the step on the East side of the house. I saw an object
         that looked like a star to start With, this is what made me notice it,
         but it was a different color. The more I looked at it 1 the bigger it
         got. It came from the East at a terrific speed. It started low on
         the eastern horizon . The front was a cherry color and looked like a
         ball, a beautiful color. The second part, the cone shaped part was
         a purple color, and then the exhaust was a white which came out in
         straight lines. The exhaust which I saw, this is what made me think
         it was an object, which had to come out at a terrific force . This
         object came directly overhead at a terrific speed, no noise and I
         couldn' t smell anything. About the size I would say, of a 5 gallon
         pail at about 10 0 feet away. As it went over the house it went out
         of sight. I ran to the South corner of the house, about 18 feet and
         it was too late. I couldn't see it anymore. This object did not
         light up the area. I saw it for about 3 or 4 seconds as it was
         travelling at a terrific speed. I have seen a satelite and it looked
         like a star, a speck, but this object, there was no comparison. I
         immediately ' phoned Moose Jaw and Regina. Wha t puzzled me the most was th
         exhaust . n

         Enquiries in the a rea t'alled to locate any other persons who may have
         sizhted this object.

                        2.          On the above date while on patrol in Seal Cove, Nfid.,
                        I was contacted by iiiiiiliiiiiiiier the Church of England 1ll Baie
                        Verte. He stated t~n in Westport the previ ous night
                        on 17 APR 69. While on his return trip frem Westport, at approximate
                        ly 7 miles east et Westport on the Westport Road, he sighted, what
                        appeared to him to be, a falling meteorite. The time was 8:40 P•••
                        He said that it appeared to be anywhere from 2 to 10 miles from hie
                        position! being extremely. bright, probably because et its nearness.
                        He descr bed the object to be et a cone~shaped figure, its centre
                        being a burning orange-red colour, surro1mded with bluet. which formed
                        the cone-shape. He estimated the object to be about 8 reet long
                        and 6 feet wide at its widest extremity. Re ~rther stated that it
                        was travelling extremely fastL heading downwards towards the earth
                        at about a 45 degree angle. He said that it was travelling in a
                        North-westerly direction and estimated the entire sighting to have
                        lasted from about 2 to 5 seconds.
                        3.          Upon returning to Baie Verts, the incident was reported
                        to Cpl. H.G. KENNEDY. Baie Verte Message No. 29 was compiled and ·
                        forwarded!via SSB, to the O.C. St. Johnla Sub/Division, 19 APR 69,
                        as per ex sting instructions.
                        4.                         is the                       the Church
                        of England in D•u•e    e. He is cons.~.u ••.-.,,u a steady and
                        reliable person, and it is felt that bis report is accurate.

            Unidentified Flying Object - Trans Canada Highway -
            Approximately 20 Miles \vest of St. John's, Nfld. - 19 MAY 69.


                                    19 MAY 69
            l.      At approximately 3.45 a . m. of the above date, Mr.
            - - of 2 11\ullock Street, St. John ' s, contacted this
            ~stated that he had observed a U.F.O. on the Trans-
            Canada Highway approximately twenty miles West of St. John's .
            2.      IIIII who sounded very excited when reporting the incident,
            stated that he had first observed the light ahead of his car on
            the Higbway,at which time it resembled a star. As he continued
            East on the Trans Canada Highway, the light disappeared momentarily
            at which time his car radio cut out, his voltage indicator went
            to "Discharge" and the car stalled . He then observed the light
            to his rear, at which time the car appeared to function normally .
            The light !ollowed him for approximately six miles, during which
            time he drove at a high rate of speed, and the light had no
            difnculty in keeping pace with him. There was no vapor trail
            visible or noise heard, other than from the car and the liM      s
            described as something unlike He had ever seen before. As
            went over the crest o£ a hill, the light disappeared momentari y
            and he then observed it proceeding over the woods in a southerly
            direction.
            3.      Mr . -       was alone in his car at the time a&d there were
            no other siniii'il'r reports received at this office. There was als'O
            no other vehicular traffic present on the highway at the time .
            4.       The following self-explanatory statement was obtained
            from Mr. tllll, with the original being held on file :-


                  At approximately 3. 05 a . m., 19 MAY 69 I was proceeding East
            on the Trans-Canada Highway at approximately 20 miles ~lest o.f
            St. John'' s, Nfld. I was alone in my car when I observed a light
            ahead of me which I thought may have been a star. I continued
            onward and the light disappeared. Suddenly, my car radio went
            dead, my alternator went to "Discharge" and the car stalled momen-
            tarily. At almost the same time I ob·! erved a bright light in my
            rear view mirror. There was no other traffic around at this time .
            The light f ollow·ed me for approximately six miles, during which
            time it seemed to change positions behind me at will, coming close
            and/ or following behind a little. I heard no nodse whatsoever.
            I drove at speeds up to 100 m. p.h. but the bright light or object
            had no trouble follo111ing me . 'fhe only way I caJ,'l describe it would
            be as a bright oval. shaped, fluorescent type light, unlike any

        2.                   Information obtained from the abo~e mentioned is as
        follows:                                                          ,,

                                                                                                 -· .....
        a)  Sighting made 4 MAR 69 at e:35 p.m. to 8:45 p.m. !.S.T •.
        b) Sky clear.
                                                                                                  . ..•
        c) As noted above.
        d) All three men were on the ice at Fish Lake near Whitehorse, Y.T •
                                                             .
        e) Object seen approximately the same time by two teenagers at
            Whitehorse, Y.T.
        f) Object first appeared as a round bluish ball. It was stationary
            at the time and the size was described as hal£ moon. The object
            split in two; one piece remaining hal£ moon size and the same
            colour and position; the other piece turned a dark orange colour
            and appeared to approach the observers and increase to moon size.
            This second object appeared to move back to object at original
            position and take on original size and colour, Both objects
            seemed to fade away completely. The position of these objects
            was about 100 above the horizon. The direction of the object
            from the observers was towards the north and no directional
            movement was noticed.
        g) The sighting lasted approximately ten minutes.
        h) Five minutes after the object disappeared, the three men were
            proceeding to shore with their Ski-Doo. At this time the entire
            area was lit up by a blue light, similar to that which would be
            given off by an arc welder. The light made objects all around
            the lake clearly visible. This flash of light lasted approximately
            two to three seconds and at this time, the Ski-Doo motor cut out
            and almost stopped for no apparent reason and started running
            normally after the flash subsided. The 0bservers indicated that
            they felt heat from this flash sim~lar to rays ot sun passing
            through window glass. Enquiries from other observers and locally
          \.i~ Whitehorse area indicated that this flash o£ light was not

                        Meteor Sighting - Watson Lake Area , Y.T. - 4 MAR 69               ( 2)


     of Alberta, Edmonton, came to the detachment office seeking
     information. He was given all information available . He advised at
     this time that the vibrations felt at -       03111'0 could have been
     caused by the meteor breaki.n g the soun~er, and 1i' so , it would
     have landed within a 30 mile radius of the camp .
                                              13 MAR 69
     7.                  Mr . BAYROCK advised that his enquiries were
     concluded. He questioned the five men at the logging camp and all
     denied that there had been any vibration. They claim all they saw was
     a bright light. Mr. BAYROCK is of the opinion that the meteor
     fragments landed w1 thin a 50 mile :t'adius of Stewart Lake, 128 degrees
     40 minutes w. and 70 degrees 36 minutes N. He considers the area too
     remote to continue the search.

                        Cst. G. J. JARED, 122616 - U.F.O. Sighting
                        Carmacks, Y.T. · 3 FEB 69


                                          3 FBB 69

       1.        At 10:00 p.m. this date while driving north towards Carmacks,
       Y.T. on highway W2, I noticed an unusual white light in the sky towards
       the Mt. Nansen area. I stopped the vehic l e at Mile 96 and observed this
       phenomenon for at least 5 minutes. I~ dodged behind the hills at
       Carmacks, and in order to get a better look, I continued throu$h the
       village, and up Coal Mine hill to Mile 106. I again observed lt for
       about 5 minutes. There was no noise, movement, or colour, just a puls-
       ating white glow. It appeared about 1/Sth. the size of the full moon
       which was also out that night. The sky was dark , with scattered wisps
       of cloud , and the other star s were clearly visible.
       2.       The light still appeared to be about lS miles away towards the
       Mt. Nansen area, and about a mile off t he ground as it cleared the local
       mountain tops. However, as we observed it, it gTew brighter, them slowly
       faded softer, and appeared to diminish in size and intensity until it
       disappeared alto$ ether. . . _ _ a - - for White Pass w~.s
       also parked at M1le 107, ~~v ed~ ject for severWl )
       hours while driving south from Pelly Cro~sing. He was interviewed , ·and
       his statement is attached. The reliability of this witness is unknown~
       Mrs. Gloria JARBD, my wife, was accompanying me at the time, and                 also
       witnessed the above described object.
                                          6   FEB 69
       3.       To date local inv ~as revealed no plausible explana·
       tion for this occurence . _ . ,, another _ _ . from White
       Pass Petroleum mentioned that he had observed a ~omenon on
       24 JAN 69. I was unable to obtain a statement from him ~~ this time,
       however he gave his nddress as 312 Ha,wkins St. , Apt. ~2 , Whitehorse, Y.T.
       The information contained in paragraphs 1 & 2 was passed to the Yukon
       Sub/Division on 4 FEB 69 for processing of the usual telex. Any further
       sightings will be subsequen tly reported in the proscribed manner.
       CONCLUDED HERE

                   Cst. G.J. JARED , N22616 · U.F.O. Sighting

                   STATEMENT OF
                                              -EX!MI'l'lONIEXCEPTlaN      11c 1)
                                              ACCESS TO INFORMATION ACT/
                                              LOt SUA l'ACCtiS Al'IHFOAMATION
                    I left Pelly Crossing at 8:30 p.m. on 3 FEB 69.                As I left
   I saw a white light, with a bit of a haze over it.           I was driving south

   to Whitehorse, and it stayed on my right side.        I   noticed it again
   at Five Finger rapids, about 9:45p.m.      I stopped and watched it for a

   while at Mile 107, for about 2 minutes, then it went real bright, then
   disappeared over the top of the mountains.      That would be about 10:00 p.m.


                    r saw the same thing about Z weeks ago, at about the same
   time when I came through from Clinton Creek.       On both occassions the
   sky was clear , there was no noise from it, no colours, just white; hard
   to judge movement as the truck was moving.      Size would be about like a
   street lamp.

                       again ahead of us. It still s eemed to be a mile ahead ot us.
                       We followed the light at the same distance tor two miles west
                       and then the road came to a dead end. The light seemed to go
                       down behind some trees and we lost sight ot it. We turned
                       around and came back east a mile and turned into our drive way.
                       Approx. 150 yds, into our drive way we stopped the truck, as
                       we saw tbe light again comming back east. We shut the truck
                       and lights of and watched the obJect tor approx. five minutes.
                       It appeared to be comming twords us and then it stopped and
                       started t o go west again. It then disappeared and appeared to
                       leave very fast. The light appeared to be an orange misty colour
                       and was cons tantly going bright and dim. It was just one big

    ~~                BEGINS! I was coming south from Yorkton in my father's truck on
                      19 H~. I was making a right hand turn approx, 6 miles from
                      Yorkton when my brother and I noticed a flashing orangy red light
                      due west of the highway. It seemed to be about 1! miles away.
                      We followed it with the truck and w w re gaining on it. It
                      appeared to be from 20 to 100 ft . above the ground, The light
                      seemed to be flashing all over the thing not in one specific area,
                      We tollowd it to the end of the road and lost sight of it on
                      the other side of a bunch of trees . We watched to see what we
                      could see t or maybe five minutes then -we turned and came toward
                      home. We made a lett ll.and into our home lane when -we saw it again
                      rising from tbe bunch ot trees. We dr ove another 15'0 to 200 Yds .
                      and t b.en shut the lights and the truck off and stepped outside .
                      It then started to rise and came to~rard us, possibly about 35' to 40
                      mph and stopped halfway in the middle of a field and it seemed
                      to b.over moving closer to the ground and then going up again.
                      A.s it -went nearer the ground the light got dimmer and as it went
                      up it got brighter. This all took place about lOtOO P.M. The object
                      then sped or to the west at maybe 3 times the speed at which -we had
                      first sav it , In my estimation it could have been 10·15' ft. in
                      diameter in which the light was flashing. I don't think it vas a

                                                                8 Feb 69
           "On Sunday Feb 2, 1969 at approximately 5:40 PM I returned
!rom Dauphin. While driving up the street I aaw three girls standing
in my yard without any coats on. The temperature was l~o below at
this time. The girls were staring towards the sky.
           UpoD entering the house I asked my eon
happened. He explained that he and the girls had se
strange in the sky.
            I started to question him on what he had saw and where it
had come from. While he was showing me through the kitchen window
a black, thin objeot appeared in the west, This object came tawarde
town. I '!latched this object !or approximately two or three minutea
then phoned the R.O.M,P.; took my oamera and went out to try and take
a picture. Outside my vision was obscured by a telephone cable.
This object tilted on it's side, eat motionless for a few seconds,
then disappeared up in the air towards the south-west."

     1.     As st ated through Camrose Telex #100 I sighted a fireball on
     the above noted date. The following observations were made and are
     listed below as set out in the Operational Manual •
     A.       The fireball was sighted on the 2 OCT 71 at approx. 10:30 P. M.
     B.       The sky was clear with l ight scatt ered clouds and a full moon.
     c.       The f i reball was observed approx. 3 miles north of Rosalind, Alta.
     D.       Two bursts wer e noted . The first burst on t he initial s i ghting
              and the second burs t occurred when the fireball appeared to burn
              out.
     E.       The initial burst compared in brightness wi th an extremely bright
              flash of l ightning . The trail of the firebal l appeared to be
              two or three times as bright as the moon.
     F.       The color of the fireball changed from a bright yellow/or8 nge
              to a greenish blue similar to a mercury vapor light .
     G.       The fireball appeared approx. two times as large as the moon
              in relation to the moon.
     H.       The fireball was observed in f l ight approx. 3 seconds .                             The trail
              remained approx. 8 to 10 seconds,
     I.       No sound was audible a s t he observation wa s made from a moving
              vehicle .
     J.       The fireball travel led from the northwes t to the southeast at
              approx . a 30° angle above the horizon and an approx. 10° down-
              ward angle ,
     K.       The trail of the f i reball grew in intens~ty after the fir~ball
              burned out for approx, ~ seconds and then faded from sight in
              another ~~ t o 5 seconds . 0n the second burst the firei all appeared
              to explode into small fragments and f 0 de out.

     is required. In that event the f i le will be re- opened and a further

                       1.          With re£erence to the above may you please be advised
                       that at 8:30 P.M . on Monday the 16 th day o£
                       call was received at this Detachment from one
                       144 Dover St., Campbellton, N. B. advising that
                       hours he had seen a mysterious object that he could not
                       and he requested that we m.a ke an attempt to identify it for him .
                        2.           Immediate ~tion revealed that at approximately
                        4:30A.M . of this date llllllll' had occasion to get out of bed
                        and he noticed an ambur light approximately 500 yards above
                        Sugarloaf Mountain, which is in the Ci! Po£!!!
                                                                     Campbellton . Since
                        this light did not resemble the stars           watched it £or a
                        few minutes . During this time the objec moved in an easterly
                        direction and in so doing came closer to -           At this time
                       ......... got his wife out of bed and both~ observed the
                       ~P.t     's it appeared to hover near the same spot. During this
                        time           counted five (5) ambur lights that appeared to be
                        pulsating.     ter a period of approximately 15 minutes the object
                        started moving toward the South and as it neared the horizon it
                        dissappeared as a streak of light ,~ described it , was
                        fast as the eye could follow." _.-aoura not determine any
                        shape to this object nor could he tell how far away it was.
                       3.       On Thursday the 19 th day of August, 1971 at approximately
                2:00 A.M . Cat . G. BARAGAR and Cat . D. MARTEL of this Detachment
                1vere on patrol in the area of Sugarloaf Mountain when they also
                noticed a mysterious light on the Bast side of the Mountain. A
       q 1I     short time later the same light was observed on the West side o£
                the P.lountain and at this time these members drove as near to the
( ~,            Mountain as they could and in so doing noticed that this obj ect
             · was moving in the same direction as they were . In view of this
             t" they kept driving in the same direction for approximately 7 miles
         J
           ~  l with the object following, or at least travelling in the same
                direction. At times this object was on the right hand side of the
                car and on other occasions it was on the left hand s ide . These
                members stopped on several occasions and when they did so the object
                stopped as well . On one occasion they stopped and turned all o£
                the lights on the P.O . out . This object also stopped and it's
                lights became very dim. The flashing red dome light of the Polic e
                car was put on and at this time two objects that resembled rabbit
                ears ( •r , V. Antenna) appeared on each side of the object. These
                rabbit ears appeared to take on a dull red colour. At approximately
                7 miles £rom the City the P,C . was turned and brought back the same
                highway it had left on . The object followed again stopping when
                they did until they reached the Sugarloaf Mountain again at which
                time it stopped . 3hortly t hereafter it dissappeared behind the
                ~!ountain and the     were unable to see it a ain.

direction. In rel.ation to the stars , it was found to be in almost a straigh

2. The object was examined by mysel.f through the detachment binoculars
which are 7 x 50 power. At tb.e time of the observation, the object appearec
to be ovalar in shape and emitted three distinct lights in a flashing
pattern. The most intense light was red which seemed to flash at different
pointe on the objeot. The bottom portion of ·the object emitted a green
flashing light whil.e the top portion emitted a wewcer amber flashing light.
The object appeared to be moving in an easterly direction and upwards in
relation to the stars. Eventuall.y the object moved further away and became
fixed in a position too far away to examine the . objeot any further.

readily. A patrol was made to the residence by myself and the object view-

closer than the previous viewing. The object was once again viewed through
the detachb1ent binocul~s and fouijd to have the same shape as previously
noted. At times , the fl.ashing would be more vivid than other times. The
time! M   a iroximately 10 :00 PM. The object was viewed further by myself

appeared that the object was revolving as a unit thus giving the flashing

appeared to move upwards rapidly and eventually oams to a fixed position

                 This is the                                                                    , Erickson,

                           On the 24th of August, 1971 , I saw an object in the sky which
                 I thousht was an airplane as there were distinct green &: red flashing
                 lighta. I got out of my car at my residence and shut off the engine.
                 I couldn' t hear an airplane and the object appeared to be fixed .
                 This was at approximately 10 :00 PM. I looked at it through my
                 binooulars(8 x 40). The object was very close. It appeared to be
                 oval shaped and revolving. The green seemed to be above the red.
                 There also appeared to be an amber colour which would flash period-
                 ically. The red was the strongest colour and was also flashing.
                 It was NNE at the time and moved slowly westward. The object also
                 appeared to be revolving. It eventually stayed in a fixed spot. I
                 quit looking about 11&00 PM.
                      On 25th August 1971, I spotted the object was much closer than
                 the le.st time I saw it last night. The colours were the same in hue
                 and brightness, however, the red flashing light seemed to flash at
                 different spots on the object. I called the RCMP at this ti.me and
                 they attended. The object moved eastward , then down and up to the
                 west. The ROMP left before it came to the west. When it appeared
                 in the westward pos1tion it was clearer again and I called the ROMP.
                 They arrived and viewed it with me again. Same observations were
                 made at this time except a white beam of light like headlights was
                 seen emitting from it southward. The object then rapidly moved away
                 and stayed fiJced in the sky. At llt00P!il, I stopped viewing the
                 object.
                       About 8 &30PM; 26- 8- 71, the obJect was seen again and was quite
                 close agai.n but not as close as the first night. The first night
                 was the cloaeat I've ever seen it. I once again called the ROMP
                 and they attended. At lO tOO PM t.he object was very faint and far
                 away.

                 WITNESS:          H. G. Slomba, Cat.              SI<rNEP t
